gemini-3.8-flash/09-incident-analysis/RESPONSE.md

Incident Analysis Response

Confidence Level: High (Proximate Cause and Failure Mechanism) / Moderate (Initial Failure Triggers)

  • Proximate Cause and Failure Mechanism (High Confidence):
    • Strong, direct log and timeline evidence demonstrates that enabling the tenant_retry_policy feature flag introduced zero-delay retries (delay_ms=0) when tenant policies evaluated to empty string (policy="").
    • This rapid retry loop triggered database connection pool exhaustion (wait_ms=5000, 300 connections reached), causing worker threads to block (dropping worker CPU from 65% to 18%) and cascading into checkout latency spikes.
    • Toggling feature_flag=false immediately restored exponential backoff (delay_ms=1000) on the stalled job (job=319), initiating recovery within two minutes.
  • Initial Triggers and Configuration State (Moderate Confidence):
    • While the propagation mechanism is clear from the logs, the underlying errors that prompted initial retries for job=81 and job=319 are not recorded in the provided logs.
    • Similarly, the reason tenant configurations yielded policy="" (whether due to missing database records, migration timing, or parsing errors) cannot be determined definitively without access to the code and configuration datastore.

Next Two Pieces of Data Requested

  1. Source Code Diff for API Release 2026.09.23.1 (Specifically Retry Policy Resolution Logic):

    • Rationale: To inspect how tenant_retry_policy queries tenant settings and computes backoff delays. Specifically, we need to inspect the code handling empty or missing policies (policy="") to confirm why it bypassed config_retry_default="exponential" and evaluated delay to 0ms.

  2. Full Application Error Logs and Stack Traces Preceding Retries (09:06:00–09:07:00 and 09:34:00–09:36:00 UTC):

    • Rationale: LOGS.txt shows result=retry and retry_budget_exhausted but omits the initial exception or error message that triggered attempt 1 for job=81 and job=319. Detailed error traces will determine whether the initial failures were caused by upstream application errors, transient database contention, third-party timeouts, or schema mismatches.

gemini-3.8-flash/09-incident-analysis/ACTIONS.md

Corrective and Preventative Action Plan

1. Immediate Containment

  1. Feature Flag Freeze: Keep tenant_retry_policy feature flag strictly disabled (false) across all environments (including US and eu-west).
  2. Worker Pool Normalization: Ensure worker replica count in eu-west is restored to baseline (40 instances) to eliminate database connection pool saturation.
  3. Database Health Monitoring: Maintain active monitoring on database connection utilization (alert threshold at >75% of 300 max connections) and worker pool wait duration (wait_ms).

2. Prioritized Corrective Actions

Action Item 1: Fix Retry Policy Default Fallback & Sanitize Delays (P0)

  • Description: Update the retry policy engine to strictly enforce safe fallbacks. If a tenant's retry policy is unset, empty (policy=""), invalid, or missing, the system must immediately fall back to the global default exponential backoff (config_retry_default="exponential", minimum initial delay >= 1000ms with jitter). Prohibit delay_ms=0 for retries across the codebase.
  • Owner (Role): Core Backend Engineering Lead
  • Verification Signals:
    • Unit and integration tests demonstrating that jobs with policy="" or unknown tenants default to exponential backoff with >0ms delay and jitter.
    • Staging environment load test under simulated job failures showing workers do not execute tight-loop retries.
    • Worker logs showing delay_ms >= 1000 when retries occur.
  • Rollback Considerations:
    • Changes are guarded by existing build and deployment pipelines; if tests fail or unexpected regressions appear in staging, revert commit prior to production release.

Action Item 2: Isolate Database Connection Pools & Implement Proxied Pooling (P0)

  • Description: Decouple customer-facing checkout API connection limits from asynchronous worker connection limits. Deploy a connection pooler (e.g., PgBouncer / RDS Proxy) with dedicated quotas: reserve guaranteed slots for API checkout traffic (e.g., minimum 100 slots) and restrict total worker pool connections so workers cannot exhaust database slots under any scaling event.
  • Owner (Role): Database Administrator / Infrastructure Lead
  • Verification Signals:
    • Connection pool metrics showing distinct pools for API and background workers.
    • Chaos test where background workers are scaled to 150 instances while API checkout response times and connection availability remain unaffected.
    • Zero db remaining connection slots reserved errors in API logs.
  • Rollback Considerations:
    • Connection proxy configuration can be hot-reloaded to adjust pool partition sizes; in the event of proxy degradation, bypass rules allow direct connection fallback with conservative connection limits per pod.

Action Item 3: Update Incident Runbooks & Worker Autoscaling Safeguards (P1)

  • Description: Update the on-call runbook for queue depth alerts: explicitly prohibit scaling queue workers when database connection pool wait errors (db pool wait exceeded) or DB connection saturations are detected. Implement automated guardrails in autoscaler policies that inhibit scale-up if database connection headroom is below 20%.
  • Owner (Role): Site Reliability Engineering (SRE) Lead
  • Verification Signals:
    • Runbook documentation reviewed and published in knowledge base.
    • Autoscaler dry-run test confirming worker scale-out is throttled when database connection pool metrics exceed safety thresholds.
  • Rollback Considerations:
    • If autoscaler inhibition prevents scaling during legitimate high-throughput demand, on-call engineers retain manual override capability via cluster config.

Action Item 4: Enforce Phased Regional Progressive Rollouts for Feature Flags (P1)

  • Description: Align global feature flag management policies to mandate staged canary rollouts across all regions (e.g., 1% -> 5% -> 25% -> 100% with automated metric gates for latency and error rates), eliminating single-step broad rollouts in primary regions such as eu-west.
  • Owner (Role): Release Engineering Lead
  • Verification Signals:
    • Feature flag configuration platform requires multi-stage schedule and metric validation checks prior to 100% enablement.
    • Audit logs verify that new flag changes cannot be flipped directly to 100% in any production region.
  • Rollback Considerations:
    • Emergency fast-kill switch retained to instantly disable any feature flag globally within 30 seconds.

Action Item 5: Structured Logging and Observability for Retry Failures (P2)

  • Description: Enrich worker retry log events to include explicit failure causes (e.g., exception class, message, upstream dependency) alongside job, tenant, attempt, delay_ms, and policy. Add alert rules for elevated frequency of retry_budget_exhausted and delay_ms == 0.
  • Owner (Role): Observability / Platform Engineering Lead
  • Verification Signals:
    • Worker logs capture the underlying error cause for retry events.
    • Alert fires in test environment when zero-delay or high-frequency retry bursts are emitted.
  • Rollback Considerations:
    • Logging format changes are backwards-compatible; log level can be adjusted via dynamic logger configuration without application restarts.

gemini-3.8-flash/09-incident-analysis/CUSTOMER_UPDATE.md

Between 09:07 and 10:18 UTC, customers in our European service region (eu-west) experienced elevated checkout latency and delays in background processing. Our US region was not affected.

Our investigation identified that an update to our retry management system caused automated background tasks to retry immediately without expected backoff delays. This created heavy contention on shared database resources, slowing down job processing and impacting checkout response times.

Our engineering team mitigated the issue by disabling the updated retry configuration at 09:43 UTC. System performance began recovering immediately, and all queued background jobs were fully processed by 10:18 UTC. All services have returned to normal operation.

To prevent recurrence, we are adding strict validation rules to guarantee mandatory delays on all retry routines and isolating critical database resources for customer checkouts. We sincerely apologize for any inconvenience caused to your operations.

gemini-3.8-flash/09-incident-analysis/INCIDENT.md

Incident Report: Queue Backlog and Checkout Latency (eu-west)

1. Impact

  • Affected Region: eu-west (European region). The US region remained healthy with no alerts fired.
  • Affected Services: Background worker queue processing and user checkout path.
  • Incident Duration:
    • Onset: ~09:07 UTC (queue depth began rising, DB pool wait warnings).
    • Customer Alerting: 09:11 UTC (job age and checkout latency alerts fired).
    • Mitigation / Recovery Start: 09:43–09:45 UTC (feature flag disabled, recovery observed within 2 minutes).
    • Full Resolution: 10:18 UTC (backlog completely cleared).
    • Total Duration: ~71 minutes (09:07 to 10:18 UTC).
  • Service Degradation:
    • Processing backlog accumulated across background jobs.
    • Checkout latency spiked due to database connection exhaustion.
    • Worker CPU dropped from 65% to 18% as worker threads blocked on database connection acquisition.
    • Total database connections reached the configured maximum of 300, exhausting available connection slots for the API.

2. Concise Timeline (all times UTC)

  • 09:02: Deployed API release 2026.09.23.1 introducing tenant-specific retry policy.
  • 09:06:58: worker-17 processed job=81 (tenant=acme), executing 3 retries with delay_ms=0 in 41ms, exhausting retry budget with policy="".
  • 09:07: Queue depth began rising in eu-west; worker CPU dropped from 65% to 18%.
  • 09:07:01: worker-22 logged WARN db pool wait exceeded wait_ms=5000 active=10 idle=0.
  • 09:11: Monitoring alerts triggered for job age and checkout latency.
  • 09:14: On-call engineer scaled worker instances from 40 to 90; queue depth rose at an accelerated rate.
  • 09:19:52: api-03 logged ERROR db remaining connection slots reserved.
  • 09:20: Database connection count reached the hard configured ceiling of 300.
  • 09:24: API deployment 2026.09.23.1 rolled back. New job creation normalized, but the existing queue backlog remained stalled.
  • 09:31: One worker pool restarted (worker-51 started with feature_flag=true). Processed jobs normally for 4 minutes before stalling at 09:35:12 on job=319 with policy="" (retries exhausted in 38ms).
  • 09:43: Feature flag tenant_retry_policy was disabled. worker-51 dynamically picked up feature_flag=false, applied default exponential backoff (delay_ms=1000) to job=319, and succeeded on attempt 2.
  • 09:45: Queue processing recovery observed across eu-west.
  • 10:18: Backlog cleared completely; all metrics returned to baseline.

3. Most Likely Root Cause

The most likely root cause is a logic defect in the tenant-specific retry policy implementation, activated by the tenant_retry_policy feature flag in eu-west:

  • When the feature flag was enabled, tenants without an explicitly defined retry policy (e.g., tenant=acme with policy="") defaulted to an unthrottled retry delay of zero milliseconds (delay_ms=0) rather than falling back to the global default exponential backoff (config_retry_default="exponential").
  • When jobs encountered transient errors, workers executed retries immediately in rapid succession (e.g., 3 attempts in 41ms).
  • This zero-delay retry loop created a thundering herd against downstream dependencies (particularly the shared relational database).
  • Database connection pools quickly saturated (active=10 idle=0, wait timeouts of 5000ms), causing worker execution threads to block. Blocked threads caused worker CPU utilization to collapse (65% to 18%) and halted queue processing, which cascaded to API checkout latency when total database connections hit the hard limit of 300.

4. Contributing Factors

  1. Unsafeguarded Default Fallback: The tenant retry policy logic failed to enforce a minimum backoff or fall back to exponential delay when a tenant's policy was empty string (policy="").
  2. Broad Feature Flag Exposure in eu-west: The feature flag was enabled broadly (or 100%) in eu-west at deployment, whereas the US region was protected by an incremental 2% rollout where no alerts fired.
  3. Counter-Productive Manual Scaling Under Resource Exhaustion: On-call scaled workers from 40 to 90 at 09:14 in response to rising queue depth. Because each worker held up to 10 DB connections, scaling increased database connection contention, accelerating exhaustion from 09:14 to 09:20.
  4. Shared Database Connection Pool Between API and Workers: Workers and customer-facing API servers shared the same database connection limit (300). When workers saturated connections, customer-facing checkout API calls were blocked (api-03 ERROR db remaining connection slots reserved).
  5. Decoupled API Rollback vs. Worker Execution: Rolling back the API deploy at 09:24 did not disable the worker-side feature flag or relieve worker DB contention, leaving the queue backlog stalled until the feature flag itself was toggled at 09:43.

5. Evaluation of Hypotheses: Evidence & Analysis

Hypothesis 1: Zero-Delay Retry Policy Bug Under Feature Flag (Primary Hypothesis)

  • Supporting Evidence:
    • LOGS.txt (09:06:58): worker-17 logged attempts 1, 2, and 3 for job=81 with delay_ms=0 and policy="", exhausting the budget in 41ms.
    • LOGS.txt (09:35:12): Following worker pool restart, worker-51 encountered job=319 with policy="" and exhausted retries in 38ms.
    • LOGS.txt (09:43:07–09:43:10): Upon setting feature_flag=false, the exact same job (job=319) received delay_ms=1000 (exponential backoff) and succeeded on attempt 2.
    • Recovery commenced within two minutes of toggling the feature flag off (09:43), and the queue cleared without further intervention.
    • US region ran identical application code but had the feature flag enabled for only 2% of tenants, generating no alerts.
  • Weakening Evidence: None. All log entries and timeline markers are consistent with this mechanism.

Hypothesis 2: API Deployment Binary Defect (Independent of Feature Flag)

  • Supporting Evidence:
    • Queue depth rise began five minutes after API deploy 2026.09.23.1 (09:02 to 09:07).
    • Rolling back API deploy normalized new job creation rate (09:24).
  • Weakening Evidence:
    • Rolling back the API at 09:24 did not resolve the queue backlog or relieve worker stalls.
    • The US region ran the same binary release without experiencing an outage.
    • Recovery only occurred when feature_flag was flipped at 09:43, demonstrating the issue was gated by the feature flag rather than binary-level API code regressions.

Hypothesis 3: Worker Fleet Capacity Deficit (Under-provisioning)

  • Supporting Evidence:
    • Queue depth was rising and job age alerts fired at 09:11.
  • Weakening Evidence:
    • Worker CPU dropped from 65% to 18%, indicating workers were blocked waiting for external resources, not CPU-bound.
    • Increasing worker count from 40 to 90 at 09:14 worsened the incident and accelerated database connection exhaustion.

Hypothesis 4: Infrastructure-Level Database Outage or Hardware Failure

  • Supporting Evidence:
    • Workers logged pool wait timeouts (wait_ms=5000) and DB connection cap was reached (300).
  • Weakening Evidence:
    • Non-retrying jobs succeeded during the incident when connection slots were available (e.g., worker-08 processed job=92 successfully in 830ms at 09:07:03).
    • Database recovered immediately upon reducing retry pressure via the feature flag without database restarts or hardware remediation.

6. Separation of Observed Facts vs. Inferences

Dimension Observed Facts Inferences
Retry Behavior Logs explicitly show delay_ms=0, policy="", 3 attempts within 41ms for job=81, and 38ms for job=319. Disabling flag logged delay_ms=1000. The code path for tenant_retry_policy=true lacks default fallback logic when a tenant's policy is undefined or empty string.
Worker CPU Drop CPU dropped from 65% to 18% at 09:07; worker-22 logged DB pool wait timeout (5000ms, active=10, idle=0). The drop in CPU utilization was directly caused by thread contention and blocking I/O while waiting for database connections.
Scaling Impact Scaling from 40 to 90 workers was followed by faster queue depth increase and reaching 300 DB connections at 09:20. Scaling workers added up to 500 connection demands against a 300-connection limit, turning a worker stall into an API checkout outage.
Regional Variance US region ran release 2026.09.23.1 with flag enabled for 2% of tenants; no alert fired. The 2% sample size in the US was insufficient to trigger a thundering herd on DB connections, whereas eu-west had high/total exposure.

7. What Remains Unknown

  1. Root Cause of Initial Job Failures: Why did job=81 and job=319 fail their initial attempts? The logs show retry attempts but omit the underlying exception/error payload (e.g., transient DB lock, network timeout, payload validation).
  2. Reason for Missing Tenant Policy: Why did tenant=acme and job=319 have policy=""? Is tenant policy configuration optional, missing from a database migration, or failing to load from a cache/datastore?
  3. Rollout Disparity Between Regions: Why was tenant_retry_policy enabled at 2% in the US but broadly/fully in eu-west? Was this an operational oversight, lack of automated rollout controls, or intentional configuration?
  4. Worker Configuration Architecture: How do workers consume feature flags vs. API deployments? (Workers dynamically refreshed feature flag state at 09:43:07 without a process restart, yet retained flag state across the 09:24 API rollback).
  5. Customer Transaction Impact: The exact count of dropped or delayed customer checkout transactions during the 09:11–09:45 window has not yet been quantified.

Confidence level

Medium-high (about 70–75%) that the tenant retry flag producing zero-delay retries and exhausting database connections is the root cause.

The confidence rests on: a directly observed zero-delay retry loop (09:06:58), a directly observed DB-pool wait (09:07:01), pool exhaustion at the configured max (09:20), the flag-disable → ~2-minute recovery correlation (09:43), and the US region's 2% rollout not alerting. This would distinguish a per-tenant misconfiguration from a code bug in policy resolution and explain the empty policy string.

deepseek-v4-pro/09-incident-analysis/ACTIONS.md

ACTIONS.md — Remediation plan

Immediate containment (done or in-flight)

Action Owner Status
Disable feature flag tenant_retry_policy globally Feature/Flag owner Done (09:43)
Roll back API deploy 2026.09.23.1 API deploy owner (on-call) Done (09:24)
Reduce worker pool to pre-incident scale (40) to relieve DB pressure Worker/Queue on-call Verify completed
Confirm queue depth / job age / checkout latency return to baseline SRE on-call Verify

Corrective actions (prioritized)

P1 — Eliminate zero-delay retries

  • Owner: Worker/Queue engineering.
  • Action: Enforce a minimum retry delay (backoff floor ≥ 100–1000 ms) and a hard cap on retry rate per tenant; fail fast when a resolved policy is empty or invalid instead of defaulting to delay_ms=0.
  • Verification signal: No retry_budget_exhausted policy="" lines; all retries log delay_ms > 0; retry rate per tenant bounded in dashboards.
  • Rollback: Config-only change; revert the floor/cap if it degrades legitimate throughput.

P1 — Bound database-connection usage in the retry path

  • Owner: Worker/Queue engineering + DB/Infra.
  • Action: Add connection-acquisition timeouts and a per-service connection budget below the global pool max (headroom for the API), plus circuit breaker on db pool wait exceeded.
  • Verification signal: db pool wait exceeded events drop to zero under load tests; API never logs "remaining connection slots reserved" during incident drills.
  • Rollback: Disable circuit breaker / restore prior pool limits.

P2 — Fix tenant-policy resolution bug

  • Owner: Feature/Flag owner (with API team).
  • Action: Root-cause why the flag yields policy="" and delay_ms=0; add validation and logging of the resolved policy per tenant at startup and on config change.
  • Verification signal: Unit/integration test covering a tenant with no explicit policy returns a safe default (exponential), not empty/zero.
  • Rollback: Ship behind the same flag; disable if behavior regresses.

P2 — Gradual rollout / canary for the flag

  • Owner: Feature/Flag owner.
  • Action: Re-enable only via canary (e.g., 1–5% of tenants, then ramp) mirroring the US-region approach that did not alert; add SLO-based automatic flag kill-switch.
  • Verification signal: Canary region stays below alert thresholds at each ramp step; kill-switch auto-disables flag if job age/latency breach.
  • Rollback: Kill-switch / manual disable restores baseline.

P3 — Harden incident response procedure

  • Owner: SRE / on-call.
  • Action: Update runbook: on queue+DB-pool incidents, do not scale workers up before confirming root cause (scaling 40→90 worsened exhaustion); prefer flag disable and bounded rollback.
  • Verification signal: Runbook reviewed and exercised in next game day.
  • Rollback: N/A (documentation).

Cross-cutting notes

  • Scaling workers up was counterproductive; document that DB pool is a shared, fixed resource.
  • API rollback alone did not clear the backlog because workers retained the flag; ensure flags and deploys are rolled back together or flag-off is the first containment step.

deepseek-v4-pro/09-incident-analysis/CUSTOMER_UPDATE.md

CUSTOMER_UPDATE.md

On September 23, some jobs in our EU-West region were delayed for roughly an hour. The issue began shortly after a new release and is now resolved, with the job backlog fully cleared.

What we know: a recently introduced setting changed how some jobs retry after a temporary failure. For a subset of customers, those retries ran with no waiting time in between, which placed unexpected load on our database. As a result, workers could not keep up and some jobs waited longer than usual to process. We turned the setting off, and processing returned to normal within a few minutes.

What we are doing: we have kept the setting disabled while we investigate, and we are adding safeguards to prevent retries from running too quickly and to protect our database capacity. We are also reviewing how we roll out this kind of change to catch issues earlier.

We are sorry for the disruption. No customer data was lost, and jobs completed normally once processing recovered.

deepseek-v4-pro/09-incident-analysis/INCIDENT.md

INCIDENT.md — Queue incident (2026-09-23)

Impact

  • Region: eu-west primarily; US region affected minimally (flag enabled for only 2% of tenants, no alert).
  • Duration: ~09:07 queue degradation began → 10:18 backlog cleared (~1h11m until recovery complete; recovery began ~09:45 after flag disable).
  • User-visible effect: job processing stalled; job age and checkout latency increased. New job creation degraded around 09:19–09:24 while database connections were exhausted.
  • Secondary effect: database connection pool exhausted at configured max of 300 (09:20), blocking both workers and the API.

Concise timeline (UTC)

Time Event (observed)
09:02 Deploy api 2026.09.23.1 — adds tenant-specific retry policy.
09:06:58 worker-17 retries job=81 (tenant=acme) 3× with delay_ms=0, exhausts retry budget in 41 ms.
09:07 Queue depth begins rising in eu-west; worker CPU falls 65% → 18%.
09:07:01 worker-22 logs "db pool wait exceeded wait_ms=5000 active=10 idle=0".
09:11 Alerts fire for job age and checkout latency.
09:14 On-call scales workers 40 → 90; queue depth rises faster.
09:19:52 api-03 logs "db remaining connection slots reserved".
09:20 DB connections reach configured max (300).
09:24 API deploy rolled back; new job creation normalizes; backlog still stuck.
09:31 One worker pool restarted; processes normally ~4 min, then stalls.
09:43 Feature flag tenant_retry_policy disabled; recovery within ~2 min.
10:18 Backlog cleared.

Most likely root cause

Enabling the tenant_retry_policy flag caused affected tenants' retry policy to resolve to a zero-delay retry (three attempts at delay_ms=0, budget exhausted in ~41 ms — see LOGS 09:06:58). This tight retry loop consumed database connections rapidly, exhausting the shared pool (max 300), which blocked workers (idle/CPU drop) and the API (new-job-creation errors), stalling the queue. The correlation with flag disable (recovery within ~2 minutes) and the US region's 2% rollout (no alert) strongly implicate the flag.

Contributing factors

  • Zero-delay retry loop (no backoff floor) for affected tenants.
  • Retry path opening/holding DB connections without a bound.
  • Flag enabled broadly in eu-west (vs 2% canary in US).
  • Scaling workers 40→90 increased connection demand and worsened exhaustion.
  • API rollback did not change the worker-side retry flag, so the backlog remained stuck after rollback.
  • No observed circuit breaker or rate limit on DB-connection acquisition.

Evidence per hypothesis

H1 — Tenant retry flag produced a zero-delay retry loop that exhausted DB connections (most likely)

Supporting:

  • 09:06:58 job=81 attempt=1/2/3 all delay_ms=0, retry_budget_exhausted policy="" in 41 ms.
  • 09:07:01 db pool wait exceeded wait_ms=5000 active=10 idle=0 — connection contention.
  • 09:20 DB connections hit max 300; 09:19:52 API "remaining connection slots reserved".
  • Worker CPU fell (consistent with blocking on DB, not busy computation).
  • Flag disabled at 09:43 → recovery ~2 min; job=319 then retried at delay_ms=1000 and succeeded.
  • US region (2% tenants, flag on) never alerted.

Weakening:

  • Only one tenant (acme) is directly observed with delay_ms=0; no evidence that all tenants hit it.
  • The mechanism by which retries consume a new DB connection is inferred, not directly logged.
  • Worker CPU drop could have other causes (though DB-pool-wait logs corroborate blocking).

H2 — Independent database connection leak / pool exhaustion unrelated to retry policy

Supporting:

  • Pool exhaustion (max 300) is directly observed.
  • Restart of one worker pool at 09:31 initially processed normally, suggesting some state cleared on restart.

Weakening:

  • Recovery tracks the flag disable (09:43), not the restart (09:31) — restart alone did not fix it.
  • Zero-delay retry loop is directly observed and is a sufficient explanation for rapid connection growth.

What remains unknown

  • How many tenants (and which) were affected by the zero-delay policy.
  • Why the policy resolved to an empty string (policy="") and delay_ms=0 — a lookup bug vs. a specific tenant config vs. a default fallback.
  • Whether each retry attempt opens a new DB connection, or whether the loop holds a connection for its whole duration.
  • Why the 09:31 worker-pool restart stalled again after ~4 minutes (presumably re-encountering the same flag, but not directly evidenced).
  • Exact volume/frequency of retry_budget_exhausted events during the peak.

Note: correlation between the flag and recovery is not proof of causation; the above is the hypothesis best supported by the available evidence.

kimi-k3/09-incident-analysis/RESPONSE.md

Response — confidence and next data requests

Confidence level

Moderate-to-high (~75%) that the flag-scoped, empty retry policy with delay_ms=0 is the root cause. The evidence is strong: the failure signature (policy="", delay_ms=0, budget exhausted in ~40 ms) appears only while the flag is enabled, an unflagged tenant succeeded during the incident, the US region at 2% flag exposure was unaffected, and disabling the flag produced recovery within two minutes with the same stuck job immediately succeeding at delay_ms=1000. Confidence is held below certainty because the logs do not record per-tenant flag state or the policy-resolution path, the DB-pool exhaustion mechanism is inferred rather than traced, and the partial effect of the 09:24 API rollback (new job creation normalized while the backlog stayed stuck) is not fully explained by the flag alone.

Next two pieces of data to request

  1. The tenant retry-policy configuration store and the flag's per-tenant evaluation records (e.g., flag evaluation logs for tenants acme and beta, and the actual policy rows/config the new code resolved). This would confirm whether flagged tenants had missing/malformed policy entries and whether resolution fell back to an empty policy instead of the documented exponential default — directly testing the root-cause hypothesis.

  2. Per-connection DB telemetry with worker/job attribution for 09:00–10:30 (connection open/hold time, wait-queue depth, and which worker/job IDs held connections). This would establish the causal chain — or refute it — between zero-delay retry loops and the pool saturation that stalled workers and starved the API, and explain why scaling workers made the backlog grow faster.

kimi-k3/09-incident-analysis/CUSTOMER_UPDATE.md

Customer Update

Between roughly 09:07 and 10:18 UTC today, customers in our Europe region experienced slow checkouts and delays in background job processing; some requests may have failed during the peak. Our US region was not affected.

The problem followed a routine update that changed how failed jobs are retried. Our current evidence indicates that, for some accounts, the new retry setting did not apply a waiting period between attempts, causing jobs to retry in a tight loop and overload a shared database resource. Our investigation is still confirming the exact details, and we will share more once verified.

Once we identified the likely cause, we turned off the new retry setting. Service began recovering within minutes, and all delayed work finished by 10:18 UTC. No data was lost.

To prevent a repeat, we are adding safeguards so retries always use a safe delay, improving earlier detection, and tightening how new settings roll out. We apologize for the disruption and appreciate your patience.

kimi-k3/09-incident-analysis/INCIDENT.md

Incident Analysis — eu-west queue backlog (2026-10-11, times UTC)

Impact (observed)

  • eu-west job queue degraded from ~09:07 to 10:18 (~71 minutes).
  • Queue depth rose while worker CPU fell from 65% to 18% (workers stalled, not busy).
  • Alerts fired for job age and checkout latency (09:11), indicating customer-facing checkout slowness.
  • DB connections hit the configured maximum of 300 (09:20); API logged "db remaining connection slots reserved" (09:19:52), so some API requests likely failed.
  • US region (same release, flag at 2% of tenants) showed no alerts.

Concise timeline (observed)

  • 09:02 — API release 2026.09.23.1 deployed; adds tenant-specific retry policy.
  • 09:06:58 — worker-17: job 81 (tenant acme) retries 3x with delay_ms=0, then retry_budget_exhausted policy="" in 41 ms.
  • 09:07 — queue depth rising; worker CPU drops 65% -> 18%; worker-22 logs DB pool wait exceeded 5000 ms. Tenant beta job succeeds normally (830 ms).
  • 09:11 — alerts: job age, checkout latency.
  • 09:14 — workers scaled 40 -> 90; queue depth rises faster.
  • 09:19–09:20 — API connection-slot errors; DB connections at max (300).
  • 09:24 — API rollback; new job creation normalizes; existing backlog stays stuck.
  • 09:31 — worker pool restart; starts with feature_flag=true; healthy ~4 min, then stalls again (job 319: retry_budget_exhausted policy="" at 09:35:12).
  • 09:43 — flag tenant_retry_policy disabled; job 319 immediately retries with delay_ms=1000 and succeeds in 190 ms; recovery begins within 2 minutes.
  • 10:18 — backlog cleared.

Most likely root cause (inference)

For tenants with tenant_retry_policy enabled, the new retry-policy resolution returns an empty policy (policy="") with zero retry delay. Retries fire instantly, the retry budget is exhausted in ~40 ms, and jobs are failed/requeued in a tight loop instead of backing off. This retry storm plus immediate requeues saturated the DB connection pool, which stalled workers (CPU collapse) and starved the API. Disabling the flag restored the default exponential policy (delay_ms=1000) and jobs succeeded immediately.

Hypotheses and evidence

H1 — Flagged tenant retry policy resolves to empty/zero-delay config (root cause)

  • Supports: policy="" and delay_ms=0 in logs; budget exhausted in ~40 ms; recovery within 2 min of flag disable with no other change; same job succeeds with delay_ms=1000 right after flag-off; US (flag at 2%) unaffected; unflagged tenant (beta) succeeded while flagged tenant (acme) failed.
  • Weakens: logs do not record per-tenant flag state, so the acme/beta flag split is inferred; we have not seen the policy-resolution code or tenant config.

H2 — DB connection pool exhaustion is the primary cause

  • Supports: pool wait exceeded at 09:07:01; connections at max 300 at 09:20; scaling workers 40->90 made things worse (more connections demanded).
  • Weakens: pool warnings appear after the first zero-delay retry events and after queue depth began rising; disabling the flag fixed everything with no pool change. More likely an amplifier/symptom than the trigger.

H3 — API release broken independent of the flag

  • Supports: onset ~5 min after deploy; rollback normalized new job creation.
  • Weakens: same release in US was fine; rollback did NOT clear the stuck backlog — the flag disable did. The release is the delivery vehicle for the policy, not obviously the fault itself.

Contributing factors (inference)

  • No minimum retry-delay floor or fallback when tenant policy resolves empty.
  • retry_budget_exhausted jobs appear to requeue/hot-loop instead of being dead-lettered (inferred from recurring failures and rising queue depth).
  • Asymmetric flag rollout: ~full enablement in eu-west vs 2% in US concentrated the blast radius in one region.
  • Scaling workers before identifying the bottleneck amplified DB contention.
  • DB pool ceiling (300) shared by API and workers allowed queue retries to starve customer-facing API traffic.

What remains unknown

  • Why the policy resolves to "" for flagged tenants: missing tenant config, parse failure, or a fallback bug in the release.
  • The exact mechanism connecting retry loops to DB pool exhaustion (connection per attempt vs. held connections while stalled).
  • Why the 09:24 API rollback normalized new job creation but the worker-side flag (not the API build) controlled backlog recovery.
  • Whether US's 2% cohort was truly unaffected or simply below alert thresholds.

nemotron-3-ultra/09-incident-analysis/RESPONSE.md

Response Summary

Confidence Level

High (90%) on root cause: the tenant_retry_policy feature flag enabled a retry policy with 0 ms delay and empty policy name, causing immediate retry exhaustion and DB connection starvation. Evidence: (1) logs show policy="" and delay_ms=0 at 09:06:58; (2) disabling flag at 09:43 produced immediate recovery with exponential backoff (1000 ms delay); (3) rollback at 09:24 did not resolve backlog while flag remained on; (4) US region with 2% flag exposure had no alert.

Medium (65%) on why the policy was empty — could be missing tenant config, broken default fallback, or code defect in policy resolution. Need code/config inspection.

Next Two Pieces of Data to Request

  1. Worker retry policy resolution code path — the function that reads tenant config, applies defaults, and produces the effective policy string and delay_ms. Specifically need to see how an empty string is produced and whether a default fallback exists.

  2. Tenant retry configuration for acme and beta (the two tenants visible in logs) — to determine whether they had explicit retry config or were relying on a broken default. Also need the global default config applied when tenant config is absent.

nemotron-3-ultra/09-incident-analysis/CUSTOMER_UPDATE.md

Customer Update — eu-west Processing Delays (2026-09-23)

Between 09:02 and 10:18 UTC, customers in the eu-west region experienced delayed job processing and elevated checkout latency. The US region was not affected.

A configuration change deployed at 09:02 introduced a new retry policy for background jobs. The policy contained a defect that caused jobs to retry immediately without backoff, consuming database connections and preventing work from completing. Our team identified the issue, disabled the feature at 09:43, and processing returned to normal within two minutes. The backlog cleared by 10:18.

No data was lost. All jobs queued during the incident have been processed successfully.

We are fixing the retry policy, adding safeguards to prevent similar storms, and updating our runbooks so that feature flags can be disabled more quickly in future incidents. We will share a detailed postmortem within five business days.

If you observed failed checkouts or stuck jobs during this window, no action is needed on your part — the system has recovered automatically. Please contact support if you see any residual issues.

nemotron-3-ultra/09-incident-analysis/INCIDENT.md

Incident Analysis: eu-west Queue Stall (2026-09-23)

Impact

  • Duration: ~76 minutes (09:02–10:18 UTC)
  • Region: eu-west (US unaffected — flag enabled for only 2% of tenants)
  • Symptoms: Queue depth grew unbounded; worker CPU collapsed from 65% → 18%; DB connections saturated at 300; checkout latency and job-age alerts fired
  • Resolution: Disabling feature flag tenant_retry_policy at 09:43 restored processing; backlog cleared by 10:18

Concise Timeline (observed facts)

Time (UTC) Event
09:02 API deploy 2026.09.23.1 (adds tenant-specific retry policy behind flag tenant_retry_policy)
09:06:58 Worker logs: job 81 (tenant=acme) retries 3× with 0 ms delay, then retry_budget_exhausted policy=""
09:07 Queue depth rises; worker CPU drops 65% → 18%
09:07:01 DB pool wait exceeded (5 s); active=10, idle=0
09:11 Alerts: job age, checkout latency
09:14 Workers scaled 40 → 90; queue depth accelerates
09:19:52 API error: "db remaining connection slots reserved"
09:20 DB connections hit configured max (300)
09:24 API deploy rolled back; new job creation normalizes, backlog persists
09:31 One worker pool restarted; processes normally ~4 min, then stalls
09:31:10 Restarted worker starts with config_retry_default="exponential" feature_flag=true
09:35:12 Same worker: retry_budget_exhausted policy="" elapsed_ms=38
09:43 Feature flag tenant_retry_policy disabled
09:43:07 Worker logs config_changed feature_flag=false
09:43:09–10 Job 319 succeeds: attempt 1 retry delay_ms=1000, attempt 2 success
10:18 Backlog cleared

Root Cause Hypotheses

H1 (Strongest): Buggy retry policy activated by feature flag

  • Evidence supporting:
    • Logs show policy="" and 0 ms delay → immediate retries exhaust budget in ~40 ms
    • Disabling flag at 09:43 caused immediate recovery; jobs then used exponential backoff (1000 ms delay) and succeeded
    • US region had flag for only 2% of tenants → no alert
    • Rollback at 09:24 did not resolve backlog (flag still on), confirming flag—not deploy artifact—was the trigger
  • Evidence weakening: None directly; rollback not fixing it is consistent with flag-driven behavior
  • Unknown: Why policy="" (empty string) — config default missing? Tenant-specific config absent?

H2: DB connection exhaustion as primary cause

  • Evidence supporting: Connections hit 300; pool waits; "slots reserved" error
  • Evidence weakening: CPU dropped before connection saturation (09:07 vs 09:20); restarting one worker pool worked briefly while DB was saturated → workers stalled due to retry loops, not DB unavailability alone
  • Assessment: DB exhaustion is a consequence of retry storms, not root cause

H3: Worker scaling worsened contention

  • Evidence supporting: Scaling 40→90 at 09:14 accelerated queue growth
  • Evidence weakening: Scaling was a response, not initiator; queue already rising at 09:07
  • Assessment: Contributing factor (amplifier), not root cause

Contributing Factors

  1. Feature flag enabled for broad tenant cohort in eu-west (vs 2% in US)
  2. No circuit breaker / retry budget enforcement at worker level
  3. DB connection limit (300) too low for retry storm volume
  4. Rollback procedure didn't include feature flag disable
  5. On-call scaling action lacked visibility into retry behavior

What Remains Unknown

  • Exact code path producing policy="" and 0 ms delay (config default? tenant override?)
  • Whether tenant acme/beta had custom retry config or fell back to broken default
  • Why restarted worker processed normally for ~4 min before stalling (cache warm-up? connection pool hysteresis?)
  • Whether any data loss / duplicate processing occurred during retry storms

nemotron-3-ultra/09-incident-analysis/TASK.md

Task 09 — Incident analysis

Analyze TIMELINE.md and LOGS.txt. Produce:

  • INCIDENT.md: impact, concise timeline, most likely root cause, contributing factors, evidence supporting or weakening each hypothesis, and what remains unknown.
  • ACTIONS.md: immediate containment plus prioritized corrective actions with owners expressed as roles, verification signals, and rollback considerations.
  • CUSTOMER_UPDATE.md: a plain-language update under 180 words. Do not claim certainty beyond the evidence or blame an individual.

Separate observed facts from inference. Avoid treating correlation as proof. Write RESPONSE.md with your confidence level and the next two pieces of data you would request. Work only in this directory.

nemotron-3-ultra/09-incident-analysis/TIMELINE.md

Queue incident timeline (UTC)

  • 09:02 deploy api 2026.09.23.1; change adds tenant-specific retry policy.
  • 09:07 queue depth begins rising in eu-west; worker CPU falls from 65% to 18%.
  • 09:11 alerts fire for job age and checkout latency.
  • 09:14 on-call increases workers from 40 to 90; queue depth rises faster.
  • 09:20 database connections reach configured maximum of 300.
  • 09:24 API deploy rolled back; new job creation normalizes, backlog still stuck.
  • 09:31 one worker pool restarted; it processes jobs normally for four minutes, then stalls.
  • 09:43 feature flag tenant_retry_policy disabled; recovery begins within two minutes.
  • 10:18 backlog cleared.
  • US region uses the same release but had the flag enabled for only 2% of tenants; no alert fired there.
